29:T4c9,<div class="lab-head"><h4>The tangent line as an approximation</h4><span class="tag">Lab · move x away from a</span></div>
<canvas class="tall" id="lin-cv" aria-label="A curve, its tangent line at a, and the gap between them at x"></canvas>
<div class="controls">
  <label for="lin-fn">function
    <select id="lin-fn">
      <option value="sqrt">√x at a = 4</option>
      <option value="exp">eˣ at a = 0</option>
      <option value="ln">ln x at a = 1</option>
      <option value="sin">sin x at a = 0</option>
      <option value="cbrt">∛x at a = 8</option>
    </select>
  </label>
  <label for="lin-dx">dx = x − a <input id="lin-dx" type="range" min="-2" max="2" step="0.01" value="1"></label>
</div>
<div class="readout">
  <div>f(x), the true value<b class="c-f" id="lin-f">–</b></div>
  <div>L(x), the tangent line<b class="c-t" id="lin-L">–</b></div>
  <div>error f(x) − L(x)<b class="c-d" id="lin-e">–</b></div>
  <div>Δy (curve) vs dy (tangent)<b id="lin-dy">–</b></div>
</div>
<p class="note">Near a, the tangent line hugs the curve, so L(x) is a great estimate. Move x farther away and the gap grows. On the graph, dy is the rise along the tangent and Δy is the real rise along the curve.</p>1b:["$","$L15","2",{"children":["$","$L28",null,{"id":"lab-linear","html":"$29"}]}]
